<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> An integer polynomial is intersective if it has a root modulo every positive integer. For each degree <i>k</i> ≥ 2, we prove that there is an exponent $`c_k\gt 0`$ such that every set $`A\subseteq\{1,\ldots,N\}`$ whose differences avoid all nonzero values $`h(1),h(2),\ldots`$, where <i>h</i> is an intersective polynomial of degree <i>k</i> with positive leading coefficient, satisfies $`|A|=O_h(N^{1-c_k})`$. The implied constant may depend on <i>h</i>, but the power-saving exponent depends only on its degree.

- **thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $k\ge2$, there is a constant $c_k\in(0,1)$ with the following property. For every intersective polynomial $h\in\mathbb Z[x]$ of degree $k$ with positive leading coefficient, there are a constant $C_h\ge1$ and an integer $N_h\ge1$ such that, whenever $N\ge N_h$ and $A\subseteq[N]$ satisfy $$(A-A)\cap h(\mathbb N_+)\subseteq\{0\},$$ one has $$\|A\|\le C_h N^{1-c_k}.$$
  - Explanation: Integer sets avoiding nonzero values of a fixed intersective polynomial have a power-saving cardinality bound, with the exponent depending only on degree.
- **local-normalization** (construction, [source](#stmt-local-normalization)): There are positive integers $D_0,M$, an integer $-D_0<r\le0$, and an intersective polynomial $$g(x)=\frac{h(r+D_0x)}{M}\in\mathbb Z[x]$$ of degree $k$ with positive leading coefficient such that, for every prime $p$, the element $w_p=(\mathfrak z_p-r)/D_0$ belongs to $\mathbb Z_p$ and $$g(w_p+X)=\sum_{j=m_p}^k b^*_{j,p}X^j, \qquad b^*_{j,p}\in\mathbb Z_p, \qquad b^*_{m_p,p}\in\mathbb Z_p^\times.$$ Thus $g$, with these chosen roots, satisfies Condition <a href="#local:unit-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="local:unit-condition">[local:unit-condition]</a>. Moreover, for every $n\in\mathbb N_+$, $$Mg(n)=h(r+D_0n),\qquad r+D_0n\ge1.$$
  - Explanation: A progression change and division normalize the first nonzero Taylor coefficient at each chosen local root to a unit while preserving positive original arguments.
- **local-family** (construction, [source](#stmt-local-family)): For every $l\ge1$, the polynomial $h_l$ belongs to $\mathbb Z[x]$, is intersective, and has degree $k$ and positive leading coefficient $a_l$. Every coefficient of $h_l$ is $O_h(a_l)$. For every prime $p$, the element $\mathfrak z_{p,l}=(\mathfrak z_p-r_l)/l$ belongs to $\mathbb Z_p$, and $$\label{local:centered-expansion} \begin{aligned} h_l(\mathfrak z_{p,l}+X) &=\sum_{j=m_p}^k b_{j,p}\frac{l^j}{\lambda(l)}X^j,\\ v_p\!\left(b_{m_p,p}\frac{l^{m_p}}{\lambda(l)}\right) &=v_p(b_{m_p,p}). \end{aligned}$$ In particular, the root multiplicity remains $m_p$, and Condition <a href="#local:unit-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="local:unit-condition">[local:unit-condition]</a>, when it holds for $h$, holds for every $h_l$ at these roots. For all positive integers $l,D$, there is an integer $i$, $0\le i<D$, such that $$\label{eq:1} \begin{gathered} h_l(Dx-i)=\lambda(D)h_{lD}(x),\\ D\mid\lambda(D),\qquad \lambda(D)\le D^k, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{rad}}(\lambda(D))=\mathop{\mathrm{rad}}(D). \end{gathered}$$ In particular, the argument $Dx-i$ is positive whenever $x\in\mathbb N_+$.
  - Explanation: The auxiliary family retains integrality, intersectivity, root multiplicity and the unit condition, and transforms exactly on progressions.
- **local-sums** (bound, [source](#stmt-local-sums)): There is a threshold $P_1>\max(k,2)$, depending only on $h$, such that for every prime $p\ge P_1$ and every $l\ge1$, all but at most $k-1$ arguments modulo $p$ are regular for $F=h_l$, and at least half are regular. If Condition <a href="#local:unit-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="local:unit-condition">[local:unit-condition]</a> holds, one may choose $P_1=P_1(k)$, uniformly over all such polynomials and chosen roots of degree $k$. For every integer $u$ with $p\nmid u$, $$\label{eq:2} \left\|\mathbb E_{x\bmod p^j}e\!\left(\frac{uF(x)}{p^j}\right)\right\| \le p^{-8\delta j}\quad(j\ge1), \qquad \left\|\mathbb E_{\substack{x\bmod p\\x\text{ regular}}} e\!\left(\frac{uF(x)}p\right)\right\| \le p^{-4\delta}.$$ The second average is normalized by the number of regular arguments.
  - Explanation: Complete character sums and regular-argument character sums decay uniformly over the auxiliary family beyond a fixed prime threshold.
- **local-small-primes** (bound, [source](#stmt-local-small-primes)): Let $$V_k=(i^j)_{0\le i,j\le k-1},\qquad T_{k,p}=\max_{1\le j\le k}v_p(j).$$ For every prime $p<P_1$, one may take $$\label{local:small-prime-choice} S_p=v_p(b_{m_p,p})+T_{k,p}+v_p(\det V_k)$$ so that for every $l\ge1$ some integer $x\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}$ satisfies $v_p(h_l'(x))\le S_p$. In general this choice depends only on the fixed $h$, its chosen roots, and $p$. Under Condition <a href="#local:unit-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="local:unit-condition">[local:unit-condition]</a>, it reduces to $$S_p=T_{k,p}+v_p(\det V_k), \qquad \det V_k=\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}j!,$$ and hence depends only on $k$ and $p$.
  - Explanation: Interpolation supplies an evaluation point with bounded derivative valuation at every small prime, uniformly in the auxiliary index.
- **local-lifting** (bound, [source](#stmt-local-lifting)): Let $F\in\mathbb Z[x]$, let $p$ be a prime, and suppose $s=v_p(F'(x_0))<\infty$ for an integer $x_0$. If $E$ is an integer with $E\ge2s+1$, then $F$ is constant modulo $p^E$ on the argument class $x_0\bmod p^{E-s}$. For every integer $j>E$, a uniform argument in that class modulo $p^j$ gives a uniform value among the residues modulo $p^j$ congruent to $F(x_0)\bmod p^E$.
  - Explanation: A finite derivative valuation gives a congruence class on which polynomial values are constant at the base modulus and uniform at higher moduli.
- **local-cycles** (bound, [source](#stmt-local-cycles)): For every prime $p$ and every positive integer $l$, there are $L$ admissible residues $r_1,\ldots,r_L\bmod p^{E_p}$ such that $r_1+\cdots+r_L=0\bmod p^{E_p}$.
  - Explanation: Admissible polynomial residues admit a zero-sum sequence of a common fixed length at every prime.
- **tuple-list-mixing** (bound, [source](#stmt-tuple-list-mixing)): Let $G$ be a fixed directed graph with no loops and with at most one edge on each unordered pair of vertices. Write $e_G$ for its number of edges. All averages below use the normalized uniform measures on the indicated label configurations. Then $$\left\|\mathbb E\prod_{x\to y\in E(G)}W(Y_y-Y_x)-c_p^{e_G}\right\| \le e_Gp^{-4\delta}.$$ If every edge joins a vertex in one list $X$ to a vertex in a disjoint list $Y$, in either direction, let $K_G(X,Y)$ denote this product of edge weights. As a kernel acting by normalized averaging between the uniform $L^2$ spaces of the two lists, $$\\|K_G-c_p^{e_G}\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le(32e_G)^{1/4}p^{-\delta}. \label{eq:3}$$ Both bounds are uniform in $l$ and $p$ and include $e_G=0$, when the products are one and both errors are zero.
  - Explanation: Products of directed edge weights satisfy counting and bipartite operator estimates under normalized uniform label measures.
- **tuple-marked-positive** (bound, [source](#stmt-tuple-marked-positive)): There is a constant $c_*>0$, independent of $l,p,S$ and the cut, such that if $b\in S$, then $$Q_S(J)\ge c_*\, \mathbb E_{Y,j_+} I_S(Y) J\bigl((Y_{u_S(v),j_v})_{v\in V_+}\bigr)^2. \label{eq:4}$$ Here $j_+$ consists of independent uniform indices on $V_+$.
  - Explanation: Marked cuts have a positive quadratic-form lower bound supplied by the index interactions.
- **tuple-unmarked-defect** (bound, [source](#stmt-tuple-unmarked-defect)): Suppose $b\notin S$. Split the address lists into the two halves of the cut. Denote the plus lists by $Y_+$, and let $I_+(Y_+)$ be the product of the weights on edges internal to them. Put $$\mathcal D_S(J)=\mathbb E_{Y_+,j_+} I_+(Y_+) J\bigl((Y_{u_S(v),j_v})_{v\in V_+}\bigr)^2.$$ If $\|S\|\le t$, then $$Q_S(J)\ge-Cp^{-\delta}\mathcal D_S(J). \label{eq:5}$$ If $\|S\|=t+1$, then $Q_S(J)\ge0$.
  - Explanation: Unmarked cuts have a controlled negative defect; terminal components have a nonnegative quadratic form.
- **tuple-marking-domination** (bound, [source](#stmt-tuple-marking-domination)): If $b\notin S$ and $\|S\|\le t$, then, with $S'=S\cup\{b\}$, $$Q_{S'}(J)\ge c_*r_*^{-n_*}\mathcal D_S(J). \label{eq:6}$$
  - Explanation: Marking another block gives a positive contribution that dominates the same half-label energy used to bound the unmarked defect.
- **tuple-laws** (construction, [source](#stmt-tuple-laws)): For all sufficiently large primes $p$, uniformly in $l\ge1$, define $$\begin{aligned} M_{l,p}&=\sum_{\substack{S\subseteq[w]\\\|S\|\le t+1}} p^{-\|S\|\delta/2}\nu_{l,p,S},\\ \mu_{l,p}&=\frac{M_{l,p}}{M_{l,p}(1)},\\ \varepsilon_{l,p}&=\frac{1}{M_{l,p}(1)} \sum_{\|S\|=t+1}p^{-(t+1)\delta/2}\nu_{l,p,S}. \end{aligned}$$ Then $\mu_{l,p}$ is a reflection-positive probability law and $0\le\varepsilon_{l,p}\le\mu_{l,p}$. Its exceptional mass satisfies $$\eta_{l,p}:=\varepsilon_{l,p}(1)\le p^{-4}.$$ The probability law $$\mu_{l,p}^{\circ} =\frac{\mu_{l,p}-\varepsilon_{l,p}}{1-\eta_{l,p}}$$ has only admissible increments $z_{v_{j+1}}-z_{v_j}$ along the designated cycle. Every component and both measures $\mu_{l,p}$, $\varepsilon_{l,p}$ are invariant under simultaneous translation of all labels. Every positive-mass component or subsum therefore has uniform single-slot marginals after normalization. In either $\mu_{l,p}$ or $\mu_{l,p}^{\circ}$, the total probability of components with $S\ne\varnothing$ is $O(p^{-\delta/2})$.
  - Explanation: A weighted mixture produces reflection-positive laws, small exceptional mass, uniform marginals and nonexceptional cycle support.
- **tuple-covariance** (bound, [source](#stmt-tuple-covariance)): For every $l\ge1$, every sufficiently large prime $p$, and every integer $D\ge1$ with $p\nmid D$, the following holds. For each $a'\in\mathbb F_p$, the map $$(z_v)_{v\in V}\longmapsto \bigl(\lambda(D)^{-1}(z_v-a')\bigr)_{v\in V} \label{eq:7}$$ pushes $\mu_{l,p}$ to $\mu_{lD,p}$ and $\varepsilon_{l,p}$ to $\varepsilon_{lD,p}$.
  - Explanation: Affine progression changes transport both the full tuple law and its exceptional measure to the corresponding auxiliary index.
- **tuple-conditional-mixing** (bound, [source](#stmt-tuple-conditional-mixing)): For all sufficiently large $p$, uniformly in $l$, let $\rho_p$ be either $\mu_{l,p}$ or $\mu_{l,p}^{\circ}$. For every $v\in V$ and every function $f:\mathbb F_p\to\mathbb C$ of uniform mean zero, $$\left\\|\mathbb E_{\rho_p}\bigl(f(z_v)\mid (z_{v'})_{v'\ne v}\bigr)\right\\|_2 \le p^{-\gamma}\\|f\\|_2. \label{eq:8}$$ The norm on the left is taken in the other-label marginal of $\rho_p$; the norm on the right is uniform on $\mathbb F_p$.
  - Explanation: Conditional expectation of a mean-zero single-label function contracts in the other-label marginal norm.
- **tuple-threshold-dependence** (bound, [source](#stmt-tuple-threshold-dependence)): For the auxiliary family and local choices above, the threshold $P$ can be chosen as a function of $k,P_1,L,t$ alone so that Proposition <a href="#tuple:laws" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tuple:laws">12</a>, Lemmas <a href="#tuple:covariance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tuple:covariance">13</a> and <a href="#tuple:conditional-mixing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tuple:conditional-mixing">14</a>, and <a href="#eq:9" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:9">[eq:9]</a> hold uniformly in $l$. For general fixed data, $P_1$ may depend on $h$, and $L$ may depend on $h$ and its chosen roots. If $h$ satisfies Condition <a href="#local:unit-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="local:unit-condition">[local:unit-condition]</a> and the degree-dependent choices of $P_1$, $E_p$, and $L$ from Section <a href="#sec:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local">2</a> are used, then $P$ and $M_0$ depend only on $k$.
  - Explanation: The prime threshold is controlled by fixed structural choices and becomes degree dependent after unit normalization.
- **lift-signed-holder** (bound, [source](#stmt-lift-signed-holder)): For every positive integer $l$, finite set $\mathcal P$ of primes at least $P$, and real functions $g,g_v:X_{\mathcal P}\to\mathbb R$, $$Z_{l,\mathcal P}(g)\ge0, \qquad \left\|\mathcal Z_{l,\mathcal P}((g_v)_{v\in V})\right\| \le\prod_{v\in V} Z_{l,\mathcal P}(g_v)^{1/d}. \label{eq:10}$$
  - Explanation: Reflection positivity gives nonnegative diagonal integrals and a signed multilinear Hölder inequality.
- **lift-seminorm** (bound, [source](#stmt-lift-seminorm)): The map $g\mapsto\\|g\\|_{l,\mathcal P}$ is a seminorm on the real functions on $X_{\mathcal P}$. It satisfies $$\|\mathbb Eg\|\le\\|g\\|_{l,\mathcal P}, \qquad \\|\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{l,\mathcal P\setminus B} =\\|\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{l,\mathcal P} \le\\|g\\|_{l,\mathcal P}.$$ Translations of the input are isometries. Moreover, if $D$ is a positive integer coprime to every prime of $\mathcal P$, then for any $a'\in X_{\mathcal P}$, $$\left\\|g\bigl(\lambda(D)^{-1}(\,\cdot-a')\bigr) \right\\|_{l,\mathcal P} =\\|g\\|_{lD,\mathcal P}. \label{lift:transport}$$ Here the affine change is interpreted separately at each prime.
  - Explanation: The diagonal root is a seminorm with mean domination, coordinate-averaging contraction, translation isometry and progression transport.
- **lift-large-denominator** (bound, [source](#stmt-lift-large-denominator)): Fix $l\ge1$ and a finite prime set $\mathcal P$ as above. At each prime choose either $\rho_p=\mu_{l,p}$ or $\rho_p=\mu_{l,p}^{\circ}$. Let $g_v:X_{\mathcal P}\to\mathbb C$ be arbitrary functions. If, in one distinguished slot $v$, every Fourier coefficient at a denominator at most $R$ vanishes, where $R\ge1$, then $$\left\|\int\prod_{u\in V}g_u(z_u)\, d\bigotimes_{p\in\mathcal P}\rho_p\right\| \le R^{-\gamma}\\|g_v\\|_2 \prod_{u\ne v}\\|g_u\\|_{2(d-1)}. \label{eq:11}$$
  - Explanation: Vanishing low-denominator Fourier coefficients in a distinguished slot force decay of the multilinear integral.
- **lift-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lift-moments)): Let $\mathcal P$ be any finite set of primes, $Q\ge1$, and let $I$ be a consecutive integer interval with $\|I\|\ge10Q^6$. For every integer $r\ge1$, every $\epsilon>0$, every function $f:I\to\mathbb C$, and every subfamily $\mathcal F\subseteq\{B\subseteq\mathcal P:q_B\le Q\}$, $$\left\\|\sum_{B\in\mathcal F}f_B\right\\|_{2r} \le C_{r,\epsilon}\\|f\\|_\infty Q^\epsilon. \label{eq:12}$$ The constant is independent of the prime set, the interval and its location, the function, and the subfamily.
  - Explanation: Every fixed even moment of a complete-support subfamily of a Fourier lift has a uniform subpower bound.
- **lift-specialization** (bound, [source](#stmt-lift-specialization)): Let $B\subseteq\mathcal P$, fix $z_B\in X_B$, and let $G:X_{\mathcal P}\to\mathbb C$. For $1\le s<\infty$, $$\\|G(z_B,\cdot)\\|_{L^s(X_{\mathcal P\setminus B})} \le q_B^{1/s}\\|G\\|_{L^s(X_{\mathcal P})}.$$ In particular, a Fourier lift or a subfamily of its complete supports satisfies <a href="#eq:12" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:12">[eq:12]</a> after specialization with the additional factor $q_B^{1/(2r)}$. The same bound holds after retaining either the remaining denominators at most a given $H\ge1$, or those greater than $H$.
  - Explanation: Fixing residue coordinates has a controlled norm cost, including after cuts on the remaining denominators.
- **lift-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-lift-density)): For all positive integers $l,N$ and all $A\subseteq[N]$, $$Y_l(N,A)\ge\left(\frac{\|A\|}{N}\right)^d\ge0.$$ For every $\epsilon>0$, there is a constant $C_\epsilon$, independent of $l,N,A$, such that $$Y_l(N,A)\le C_\epsilon N^\epsilon.$$
  - Explanation: The density functional dominates a fixed power of relative density and has a uniform subpower upper bound.
- **kernel-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-kernel-estimate)): Use the fixed parameters in <a href="#eq:14" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:14">[eq:14]</a>, and let $N$ be a sufficiently large integer. Suppose $1\le l\le N^\zeta$ and $$D=M_0\prod_{p\in B}p\le N^\beta,$$ where $B$ is a finite set of primes at least $P$. Let $J_1,J_2:[N]\to\mathbb C$ satisfy $\|J_1\|,\|J_2\|\le1$ and be supported in residue classes $a_1,a_2$ modulo $D$, respectively. Suppose that $r=a_2-a_1$ is admissible for $l$ modulo $D$, that $x<y$ whenever $x\in\operatorname{supp}J_1$ and $y\in\operatorname{supp}J_2$, and that no such difference $y-x$ belongs to $F(\mathbb N_+)$. Put $H=N^{\beta/2}$ and $$\Xi=\{\xi\in\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z:q(\xi)\le H,\quad q(\xi)\text{ squarefree}, \quad(q(\xi),D)=1\}.$$ Then $$D\left\|\sum_{\xi\in\Xi}\Gamma(\xi) \widehat{J_1}_{[N]}(-\xi)\widehat{J_2}_{[N]}(\xi)\right\| \ll H^{-\gamma/2}. \label{eq:16}$$ The implied constant and the threshold for $N$ depend only on the fixed polynomial and the fixed parameters, not on $l,D,r,J_1,J_2$.
  - Explanation: Ordered supports avoiding polynomial differences force cancellation in the low-denominator pair-multiplier sum.
- **kernel-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-kernel-comparison)): For the kernels just constructed, $$\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb R/\mathbb Z}\|S_1(\alpha)-S_0(\alpha)\| \ll H^{-\gamma/2}, \label{eq:18}$$ uniformly in the data of Proposition <a href="#kernel:estimate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="kernel:estimate">22</a>.
  - Explanation: The polynomial-supported and model kernels have uniformly close Fourier transforms.
- **kernel-weyl** (bound, [source](#stmt-kernel-weyl)): If $\alpha$ lies on the minor arcs, then, for every real $y'$ and every integer interval $I\subseteq(x_*,X]$, $$\left\|\sum_{m\in I}e\bigl(\alpha F(m)+y'm\bigr)\right\| \ll U N^{-\nu}. \label{eq:21}$$
  - Explanation: Polynomial character sums on minor arcs decay uniformly over intervals, including arbitrary linear twists.
- **kernel-coefficients** (bound, [source](#stmt-kernel-coefficients)): For every $\alpha_0\in\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, write $\alpha_0=\alpha_D+\alpha_1$ for the unique decomposition in which $q(\alpha_D)$ has only prime factors dividing $D$ and $(q(\alpha_1),D)=1$. Then the coefficients defined by <a href="#eq:20" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:20">[eq:20]</a> and <a href="#eq:23" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:23">[eq:23]</a> satisfy $$\|c(\alpha_0)-c_0(\alpha_0)\|\le H^{-\gamma/2}.$$ In fact they agree when $q(\alpha_1)\le H$.
  - Explanation: Rational coefficients agree at small remaining denominators and have a power-saving difference at all other denominators.
- **transfer-specialization** (bound, [source](#stmt-transfer-specialization)): Let $I\subseteq[N]$ be a nonempty integer interval, let $B\subseteq\mathcal P_N$, and put $D=M_0q_B$. Fix $s\in\mathbb Z/M_0\mathbb Z$ and $b=(b_p)_{p\in B}\in X_B$. Let $g$ be the function on $X_{\mathcal P_N\setminus B}$ obtained by specializing $\mathcal L_{I,\mathcal P_N,Q}f_s$ at $z_B=b$. For every remaining-coordinate frequency $\xi$ with $q(\xi)q_B\le Q$, one has $$\label{eq:25} \widehat g(\xi) =\frac{D}{\|I\|} \sum_{\substack{x\in I\\x\equiv a'\pmod D}} \mathbf 1_A(x)e(-x\xi),$$ where $a'\pmod D$ is determined by $a'\equiv s\pmod{M_0}$ and $a'\equiv b_p\pmod p$ for $p\in B$.
  - Explanation: Specializing residue coordinates gives exact Fourier coefficients of an indicator restricted to the corresponding progression.
- **transfer-shorter-scale** (bound, [source](#stmt-transfer-shorter-scale)): Let $N$ be sufficiently large, let $l\ge1$, and let $A\subseteq[N]$ satisfy <a href="#eq:24" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:24">[eq:24]</a>. Suppose that $B\subseteq\mathcal P_N$ and $q_B\le N^\tau$. Set $$\begin{gathered} \mathcal S=\mathcal P_N\setminus B, \qquad D=M_0q_B, \qquad L_D=\lambda(D),\\ n_B=\left\lceil\frac{N}{L_D}\right\rceil, \qquad \Lambda_B=\frac{L_Dn_B}{N}. \end{gathered}$$ For each $v\in V$, choose $s_v\in\mathbb Z/M_0\mathbb Z$ and specialize $G_{s_v}$ at an arbitrary vector of coordinates on $B$, obtaining a function $g_v$ on $X_{\mathcal S}$. Then $$\label{eq:26} \bigl\|\mathcal Z_{l,\mathcal S}((g_v)_{v\in V})\bigr\| \le \Lambda_B^d\mathcal Y_{lD}(n_B)+O(N^{-\sigma}).$$ The scale and the rounding factor satisfy $1\le n_B<N$ and $$\label{eq:27} 1\le\Lambda_B\le1+M_0^kN^{k\tau-1}.$$ The implied constant and the threshold for $N$ are independent of $l,A,B$ and all specializations.
  - Explanation: Specialized multilinear forms are bounded by a shorter-scale maximal functional with a rounding factor tending to unity.
- **transfer-good-assignments** (bound, [source](#stmt-transfer-good-assignments)): For every $l\ge1$, the proportion $\rho_l$ of good assignments under independent uniform choices of all the $s_v$ satisfies $$\rho_l\ge\rho_*:=M_0^{1-L}>0.$$
  - Explanation: Good small-prime residue assignments have a fixed positive lower proportion across the auxiliary family.
- **transfer-cancellation** (bound, [source](#stmt-transfer-cancellation)): Let $N$ be sufficiently large, let $l\le N^\zeta$, and let $A\subseteq[N]$ satisfy <a href="#eq:24" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:24">[eq:24]</a>. If $(s_v)_{v\in V}$ is good for $l$, then $$\label{eq:29} \left\| \int\prod_{v\in V}G_{s_v}(z_v)\, d\bigotimes_{p\in\mathcal P_N} (\mu_{l,p}-\varepsilon_{l,p}) \right\|\ll N^{-\sigma}.$$ The bound is uniform in $l,A$ and the good assignment.
  - Explanation: Good residue assignments give power-saving cancellation under the product of nonexceptional tuple measures.
- **induction-recurrence** (bound, [source](#stmt-induction-recurrence)): There are constants $C_1\ge1$ and $N_1\ge3$, depending only on the fixed polynomial $h$, its chosen roots, and the fixed structural choices, such that for every integer $N\ge N_1$ and every positive integer $l\le N^\zeta$, $$\begin{split} \mathcal Y_l(N)\le C_1N^{-\sigma} +(1+u_N)\Bigg(& (1-\rho_l)\mathcal Y_{lD_\varnothing}(n_\varnothing)\\ &+\rho_l \sum_{\substack{\varnothing\ne B\subseteq\mathcal P_N\\q_B\le N^\tau}} \eta_{l,B}\mathcal Y_{lD_B}(n_B)\Bigg), \end{split} \label{eq:31}$$ where $$u_N=\bigl(1+M_0^kN^{k\tau-1}\bigr)^d-1\longrightarrow0.$$ Every scale $n_B$ appearing on the right belongs to $[1,N-1]$.
  - Explanation: The maximal density functional satisfies a recurrence using smaller interval scales and enlarged auxiliary indices.
- **induction-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-induction-decay)): There are constants $a_0,b_0>0$ and $C_0\ge1$, depending only on the fixed polynomial $h$, its chosen roots, and the fixed structural choices, such that for all positive integers $l,N$, $$\mathcal Y_l(N)\le C_0l^{b_0}N^{-a_0}. \label{eq:33}$$ The exponents $a_0,b_0$ can be chosen as functions only of the structural parameters $k,\zeta,\sigma,M_0,\rho_*$. If the chosen roots of $h$ satisfy Condition <a href="#local:unit-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="local:unit-condition">[local:unit-condition]</a> and the choices depending only on $k$ from Sections <a href="#sec:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local">2</a> and <a href="#sec:tuples" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tuples">3</a> are used, then $a_0,b_0,d$ depend only on $k$, while $C_0$ may still depend on the fixed data.
  - Explanation: Simultaneous induction over the interval scale bounds the entire auxiliary family by a power decay with controlled auxiliary-index growth.

For every prime $p$, the element $\\mathfrak z_{p,l}=(\\mathfrak z_p-r_l)/l$ belongs to $\\mathbb Z_p$, and $$\\label{local:centered-expansion}\n \\begin{aligned}\n h_l(\\mathfrak z_{p,l}+X)\n   &=\\sum_{j=m_p}^k b_{j,p}\\frac{l^j}{\\lambda(l)}X^j,\\\\\n v_p\\!\\left(b_{m_p,p}\\frac{l^{m_p}}{\\lambda(l)}\\right)\n   &=v_p(b_{m_p,p}).\n \\end{aligned}$$ In particular, the root multiplicity remains $m_p$, and Condition <a href=\"#local:unit-condition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"local:unit-condition\">[local:unit-condition]</a>, when it holds for $h$, holds for every $h_l$ at these roots. For all positive integers $l,D$, there is an integer $i$, $0\\le i<D$, such that $$\\label{eq:1}\n \\begin{gathered}\n h_l(Dx-i)=\\lambda(D)h_{lD}(x),\\\\\n D\\mid\\lambda(D),\\qquad \\lambda(D)\\le D^k,\n \\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{rad}}(\\lambda(D))=\\mathop{\\mathrm{rad}}(D).\n \\end{gathered}$$ In particular, the argument $Dx-i$ is positive whenever $x\\in\\mathbb N_+$.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-local-sums",
        "exact_quote": "There is a threshold $P_1>\\max(k,2)$, depending only on $h$, such that for every prime $p\\ge P_1$ and every $l\\ge1$, all but at most $k-1$ arguments modulo $p$ are regular for $F=h_l$, and at least half are regular. If Condition <a href=\"#local:unit-condition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"local:unit-condition\">[local:unit-condition]</a> holds, one may choose $P_1=P_1(k)$, uniformly over all such polynomials and chosen roots of degree $k$. For every integer $u$ with $p\\nmid u$, $$\\label{eq:2}\n \\left|\\mathbb E_{x\\bmod p^j}e\\!\\left(\\frac{uF(x)}{p^j}\\right)\\right|\n \\le p^{-8\\delta j}\\quad(j\\ge1),\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\mathbb E_{\\substack{x\\bmod p\\\\x\\text{ regular}}}\n        e\\!\\left(\\frac{uF(x)}p\\right)\\right|\n \\le p^{-4\\delta}.$$ The second average is normalized by the number of regular arguments.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-local-small-primes",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$V_k=(i^j)_{0\\le i,j\\le k-1},\\qquad\n T_{k,p}=\\max_{1\\le j\\le k}v_p(j).$$ For every prime $p<P_1$, one may take $$\\label{local:small-prime-choice}\n S_p=v_p(b_{m_p,p})+T_{k,p}+v_p(\\det V_k)$$ so that for every $l\\ge1$ some integer $x\\in\\{0,\\ldots,k-1\\}$ satisfies $v_p(h_l'(x))\\le S_p$. In general this choice depends only on the fixed $h$, its chosen roots, and $p$. Under Condition <a href=\"#local:unit-condition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"local:unit-condition\">[local:unit-condition]</a>, it reduces to $$S_p=T_{k,p}+v_p(\\det V_k),\n \\qquad \\det V_k=\\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}j!,$$ and hence depends only on $k$ and $p$.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-182.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-local-lifting",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F\\in\\mathbb Z[x]$, let $p$ be a prime, and suppose $s=v_p(F'(x_0))<\\infty$ for an integer $x_0$. If $E$ is an integer with $E\\ge2s+1$, then $F$ is constant modulo $p^E$ on the argument class $x_0\\bmod p^{E-s}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tuple-list-mixing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a fixed directed graph with no loops and with at most one edge on each unordered pair of vertices. Write $e_G$ for its number of edges. All averages below use the normalized uniform measures on the indicated label configurations. Then $$\\left|\\mathbb E\\prod_{x\\to y\\in E(G)}W(Y_y-Y_x)-c_p^{e_G}\\right|\n       \\le e_Gp^{-4\\delta}.$$ If every edge joins a vertex in one list $X$ to a vertex in a disjoint list $Y$, in either direction, let $K_G(X,Y)$ denote this product of edge weights. As a kernel acting by normalized averaging between the uniform $L^2$ spaces of the two lists, $$\\|K_G-c_p^{e_G}\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n       \\le(32e_G)^{1/4}p^{-\\delta}.\n \\label{eq:3}$$ Both bounds are uniform in $l$ and $p$ and include $e_G=0$, when the products are one and both errors are zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-tuple-list-mixing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a fixed directed graph with no loops and with at most one edge on each unordered pair of vertices.",

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-tuple-unmarked-defect",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $b\\notin S$. Split the address lists into the two halves of the cut. Denote the plus lists by $Y_+$, and let $I_+(Y_+)$ be the product of the weights on edges internal to them.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tuple-laws",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large primes $p$, uniformly in $l\\ge1$, define $$\\begin{aligned}\n M_{l,p}&=\\sum_{\\substack{S\\subseteq[w]\\\\|S|\\le t+1}}\n                 p^{-|S|\\delta/2}\\nu_{l,p,S},\\\\\n \\mu_{l,p}&=\\frac{M_{l,p}}{M_{l,p}(1)},\\\\\n \\varepsilon_{l,p}&=\\frac{1}{M_{l,p}(1)}\n       \\sum_{|S|=t+1}p^{-(t+1)\\delta/2}\\nu_{l,p,S}.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Then $\\mu_{l,p}$ is a reflection-positive probability law and $0\\le\\varepsilon_{l,p}\\le\\mu_{l,p}$. Its exceptional mass satisfies $$\\eta_{l,p}:=\\varepsilon_{l,p}(1)\\le p^{-4}.$$ The probability law $$\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}\n       =\\frac{\\mu_{l,p}-\\varepsilon_{l,p}}{1-\\eta_{l,p}}$$ has only admissible increments $z_{v_{j+1}}-z_{v_j}$ along the designated cycle. Every component and both measures $\\mu_{l,p}$, $\\varepsilon_{l,p}$ are invariant under simultaneous translation of all labels. Every positive-mass component or subsum therefore has uniform single-slot marginals after normalization. In either $\\mu_{l,p}$ or $\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}$, the total probability of components with $S\\ne\\varnothing$ is $O(p^{-\\delta/2})$.",

      },

        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $p$, uniformly in $l$, let $\\rho_p$ be either $\\mu_{l,p}$ or $\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tuple-threshold-dependence",
        "exact_quote": "For the auxiliary family and local choices above, the threshold $P$ can be chosen as a function of $k,P_1,L,t$ alone so that Proposition <a href=\"#tuple:laws\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"tuple:laws\">12</a>, Lemmas <a href=\"#tuple:covariance\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"tuple:covariance\">13</a> and <a href=\"#tuple:conditional-mixing\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"tuple:conditional-mixing\">14</a>, and <a href=\"#eq:9\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:9\">[eq:9]</a> hold uniformly in $l$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lift-signed-holder",
        "exact_quote": "For every positive integer $l$, finite set $\\mathcal P$ of primes at least $P$, and real functions $g,g_v:X_{\\mathcal P}\\to\\mathbb R$, $$Z_{l,\\mathcal P}(g)\\ge0,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\mathcal Z_{l,\\mathcal P}((g_v)_{v\\in V})\\right|\n \\le\\prod_{v\\in V} Z_{l,\\mathcal P}(g_v)^{1/d}.\n \\label{eq:10}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-182.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lift-signed-holder",
          "exact_quote": "For every positive integer $l$, finite set $\\mathcal P$ of primes at least $P$, and real functions $g,g_v:X_{\\mathcal P}\\to\\mathbb R$, $$Z_{l,\\mathcal P}(g)\\ge0,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\mathcal Z_{l,\\mathcal P}((g_v)_{v\\in V})\\right|\n \\le\\prod_{v\\in V} Z_{l,\\mathcal P}(g_v)^{1/d}.\n \\label{eq:10}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-182.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every positive integer $l$, finite set $\\mathcal P$ of primes at least $P$, and real functions $g,g_v:X_{\\mathcal P}\\to\\mathbb R$, $$Z_{l,\\mathcal P}(g)\\ge0,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\mathcal Z_{l,\\mathcal P}((g_v)_{v\\in V})\\right|\n \\le\\prod_{v\\in V} Z_{l,\\mathcal P}(g_v)^{1/d}.\n \\label{eq:10}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lift-seminorm",
        "exact_quote": "The map $g\\mapsto\\|g\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}$ is a seminorm on the real functions on $X_{\\mathcal P}$. It satisfies $$|\\mathbb Eg|\\le\\|g\\|_{l,\\mathcal P},\n \\qquad\n \\|\\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{l,\\mathcal P\\setminus B}\n =\\|\\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}\n \\le\\|g\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}.$$ Translations of the input are isometries. Moreover, if $D$ is a positive integer coprime to every prime of $\\mathcal P$, then for any $a'\\in X_{\\mathcal P}$, $$\\left\\|g\\bigl(\\lambda(D)^{-1}(\\,\\cdot-a')\\bigr)\n       \\right\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}\n =\\|g\\|_{lD,\\mathcal P}.\n \\label{lift:transport}$$ Here the affine change is interpreted separately at each prime.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lift-seminorm",
          "exact_quote": "The map $g\\mapsto\\|g\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}$ is a seminorm on the real functions on $X_{\\mathcal P}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The map $g\\mapsto\\|g\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}$ is a seminorm on the real functions on $X_{\\mathcal P}$. It satisfies $$|\\mathbb Eg|\\le\\|g\\|_{l,\\mathcal P},\n \\qquad\n \\|\\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{l,\\mathcal P\\setminus B}\n =\\|\\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}\n \\le\\|g\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}.$$ Translations of the input are isometries. Moreover, if $D$ is a positive integer coprime to every prime of $\\mathcal P$, then for any $a'\\in X_{\\mathcal P}$, $$\\left\\|g\\bigl(\\lambda(D)^{-1}(\\,\\cdot-a')\\bigr)\n       \\right\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}\n =\\|g\\|_{lD,\\mathcal P}.\n \\label{lift:transport}$$ Here the affine change is interpreted separately at each prime.",
      "explanation": "The diagonal root is a seminorm with mean domination, coordinate-averaging contraction, translation isometry and progression transport.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

Moreover, if $D$ is a positive integer coprime to every prime of $\\mathcal P$, then for any $a'\\in X_{\\mathcal P}$, $$\\left\\|g\\bigl(\\lambda(D)^{-1}(\\,\\cdot-a')\\bigr)\n       \\right\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}\n =\\|g\\|_{lD,\\mathcal P}.\n \\label{lift:transport}$$ Here the affine change is interpreted separately at each prime.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lift-large-denominator",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $l\\ge1$ and a finite prime set $\\mathcal P$ as above. At each prime choose either $\\rho_p=\\mu_{l,p}$ or $\\rho_p=\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}$. Let $g_v:X_{\\mathcal P}\\to\\mathbb C$ be arbitrary functions. If, in one distinguished slot $v$, every Fourier coefficient at a denominator at most $R$ vanishes, where $R\\ge1$, then $$\\left|\\int\\prod_{u\\in V}g_u(z_u)\\,\n                  d\\bigotimes_{p\\in\\mathcal P}\\rho_p\\right|\n \\le R^{-\\gamma}\\|g_v\\|_2\n                     \\prod_{u\\ne v}\\|g_u\\|_{2(d-1)}.\n \\label{eq:11}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-182.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lift-large-denominator",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $l\\ge1$ and a finite prime set $\\mathcal P$ as above.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.1"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-lift-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal P$ be any finite set of primes, $Q\\ge1$, and let $I$ be a consecutive integer interval with $|I|\\ge10Q^6$. For every integer $r\\ge1$, every $\\epsilon>0$, every function $f:I\\to\\mathbb C$, and every subfamily $\\mathcal F\\subseteq\\{B\\subseteq\\mathcal P:q_B\\le Q\\}$, $$\\left\\|\\sum_{B\\in\\mathcal F}f_B\\right\\|_{2r}\n \\le C_{r,\\epsilon}\\|f\\|_\\infty Q^\\epsilon.\n \\label{eq:12}$$ The constant is independent of the prime set, the interval and its location, the function, and the subfamily.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lift-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P$, fix $z_B\\in X_B$, and let $G:X_{\\mathcal P}\\to\\mathbb C$. For $1\\le s<\\infty$, $$\\|G(z_B,\\cdot)\\|_{L^s(X_{\\mathcal P\\setminus B})}\n \\le q_B^{1/s}\\|G\\|_{L^s(X_{\\mathcal P})}.$$ In particular, a Fourier lift or a subfamily of its complete supports satisfies <a href=\"#eq:12\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:12\">[eq:12]</a> after specialization with the additional factor $q_B^{1/(2r)}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-kernel-estimate",
        "exact_quote": "Use the fixed parameters in <a href=\"#eq:14\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:14\">[eq:14]</a>, and let $N$ be a sufficiently large integer. Suppose $1\\le l\\le N^\\zeta$ and $$D=M_0\\prod_{p\\in B}p\\le N^\\beta,$$ where $B$ is a finite set of primes at least $P$. Let $J_1,J_2:[N]\\to\\mathbb C$ satisfy $|J_1|,|J_2|\\le1$ and be supported in residue classes $a_1,a_2$ modulo $D$, respectively. Suppose that $r=a_2-a_1$ is admissible for $l$ modulo $D$, that $x<y$ whenever $x\\in\\operatorname{supp}J_1$ and $y\\in\\operatorname{supp}J_2$, and that no such difference $y-x$ belongs to $F(\\mathbb N_+)$. Put $H=N^{\\beta/2}$ and $$\\Xi=\\{\\xi\\in\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z:q(\\xi)\\le H,\\quad q(\\xi)\\text{ squarefree},\n                         \\quad(q(\\xi),D)=1\\}.$$ Then $$D\\left|\\sum_{\\xi\\in\\Xi}\\Gamma(\\xi)\n          \\widehat{J_1}_{[N]}(-\\xi)\\widehat{J_2}_{[N]}(\\xi)\\right|\n \\ll H^{-\\gamma/2}.\n \\label{eq:16}$$ The implied constant and the threshold for $N$ depend only on the fixed polynomial and the fixed parameters, not on $l,D,r,J_1,J_2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-182.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Use the fixed parameters in <a href=\"#eq:14\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:14\">[eq:14]</a>, and let $N$ be a sufficiently large integer. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Intersective polynomial** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We call $h$ *intersective* if, for every integer $q\ge1$, some integer $t$ satisfies $h(t)\equiv0\pmod q$.
- **Positive integers, interval and difference set** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $\mathbb N_+=\{1,2,\ldots\}$, $[N]=\{1,\ldots,N\}$, and $A-A=\{a-b:a,b\in A\}$.
- **Auxiliary scaling function** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Define a completely multiplicative function $\lambda:\mathbb N_+\to\mathbb N_+$ by $\lambda(p)=p^{m_p}$.
- **Auxiliary polynomial and leading coefficient** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For each $l\ge1$, let $r_l$ be the unique integer in $(-l,0]$ satisfying $$r_l\equiv\mathfrak z_p\pmod{p^{v_p(l)}} \qquad(p\mid l),$$ and set $$h_l(x)=\frac{h(r_l+lx)}{\lambda(l)}, \qquad a_l=\frac{a l^k}{\lambda(l)}.$$
- **Regular argument** ([source](#pm-h-13)): An argument $x\in\mathbb F_p$ is called *regular for $h_l$* if $h_l'(x)\ne0$ in $\mathbb F_p$.
- **Reflection positivity** ([source](#pm-h-17)): We call $\nu$ *reflection positive* if this matrix is symmetric positive semidefinite at every cut.
- **Regular-fiber edge weight** ([source](#pm-h-17)): Define $$W(y)=W_{l,p}(y)=\frac1k \bigl\|\{x\in\mathbb F_p:F(x)=y,\ F'(x)\ne0\}\bigr\|, \qquad c_p=\mathbb E_{y\in\mathbb F_p}W(y).$$
- **Product residue space and tuple law** ([source](#sec:lifts)): Set $$X_{\mathcal P}=\prod_{p\in\mathcal P}\mathbb F_p, \qquad \mu_{l,\mathcal P}=\bigotimes_{p\in\mathcal P}\mu_{l,p}.$$
- **Fourier lift** ([source](#pm-h-26)): For $Q\ge1$, the Fourier lift is $$\mathcal L_{I,\mathcal P,Q}f(z) =\sum_{\substack{B\subseteq\mathcal P\\q_B\le Q}}f_B(z) =\sum_{\substack{\xi\text{ on }X_{\mathcal P}\\q(\xi)\le Q}} \widehat f_I(\xi)e(z\xi).$$
- **Density functional** ([source](#pm-h-27)): For $A\subseteq[N]$, define $$Y_l(N,A)=Z_{l,\mathcal P_N} \bigl(\mathcal L_{[N],\mathcal P_N,Q}\mathbf 1_A\bigr).$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **normalize** [claim](#stmt-local-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-normalization): Taylor expansions and the Chinese remainder theorem -> Polynomial satisfying the unit condition
- **family** [claim](#stmt-local-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-family): Compatible root residues and completely multiplicative scaling -> Progression-compatible auxiliary polynomials
- **local-sums** [claim](#stmt-local-sums) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-sums): Repeated differencing and interpolation -> Uniform character-sum decay
- **lifting** [claim](#stmt-local-lifting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-lifting): Taylor expansion and a bijection on prime-power residue rings -> Uniform distribution among lifts
- **cycles** [claim](#stmt-local-cycles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-cycles): Divisibility at small primes and Fourier inversion at large primes -> Fixed-length admissible zero-sum sequences
- **tuple-laws** [claim](#stmt-tuple-laws) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tuple-laws): Pairing marked and unmarked contributions across each reflection -> Reflection-positive tuple laws with nonexceptional cycle support
- **holder** [claim](#stmt-lift-signed-holder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lift-signed-holder): Reflection Cauchy–Schwarz and finite sorting folds -> Signed multilinear Hölder inequality
- **moments** [claim](#stmt-lift-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lift-moments): Conditional energy, square functions and transversal coloring -> Uniform even-moment bounds
- **kernel** [claim](#stmt-kernel-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kernel-coefficients-3): Polynomial-supported signed kernel and Fourier comparison -> Cancellation of the model bilinear expression
- **scale** [claim](#stmt-transfer-shorter-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-shorter-scale): Specialization, progression covariance and seminorm comparison -> Shorter-scale maximal-functional estimate
- **cancel** [claim](#stmt-transfer-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-cancellation): Interval ordering, denominator truncation and conditioning -> Nonexceptional cancellation
- **recurrence** [claim](#stmt-induction-recurrence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-induction-recurrence): Exceptional-measure expansion and averaging residue assignments -> Recurrence at smaller interval scales
- **decay** [claim](#stmt-induction-decay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-induction-decay): Strong induction simultaneously over auxiliary indices -> Power decay with auxiliary-index growth
- **main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-induction-decay-2): Normalize, dominate density, and partition into progressions -> Degree-dependent power-saving density bound

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-LIFT**: An admissible argument class has a constant value at the specified modulus and a uniform distribution among its higher value lifts.
- **I-DEGREE**: The density saving exponent is common to fixed polynomials of the same degree; the multiplicative constant and scale threshold may depend on the polynomial.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-THRESHOLD**: Apply uniform lifting on an arbitrary argument class. -> The derivative valuation and lifting exponent conditions have not been established.
- **A-UNIFORM**: Treat the asymptotic density bound as a uniform numerical capacity guarantee over changing polynomials. -> The multiplicative constant and threshold depend on the fixed polynomial.
- **A-CERTIFICATE**: Use finite residue checks as a certificate of the density theorem. -> The finite checks cover local lifting, whereas the density theorem quantifies over intersective polynomials and avoiding integer sets.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Uniform lifting on a polynomial argument class is finite and constructive at each fixed prime-power modulus. The executable example checks this local result; the asymptotic density conclusion has separate scope.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of cases [ascending integer coefficients, prime p, x0, E, j].
# PRECONDITIONS: bounded finite contract; finite derivative valuation s; E >= 2*s+1; j > E.
# OUTPUTS: sparse exact value histograms, or REJECTED for a failed precondition.
# TIME: O(sum(p**j * degree)) exact integer operations over admitted cases.
# SPACE: O(sum(p**j)) integer histogram entries.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpz exact integers; integer modular arithmetic.
# FAILURE: invalid mathematical preconditions return REJECTED; malformed input raises an error.
import json,sys
from flint import fmpz

def solve(c):
    coeff,p,x0,E,j=c
    assert p in (2,3,5) and 2 <= len(coeff) <= 4
    assert all(type(a) is int and abs(a)<=5 for a in coeff)
    assert type(x0) is int and abs(x0)<=5
    assert 0<=E<=5 and 0<=j<=6 and p**j<=729
    def value(x):
        acc=fmpz(0)
        for a in reversed(coeff):
            acc=acc*x+a
        return int(acc)
    der=sum(i*coeff[i]*x0**(i-1) for i in range(1,len(coeff)))
    if der==0:
        return 'REJECTED'
    s=0
    while der%p==0:
        der//=p
        s+=1
    if E < 2*s+1 or j<=E:
        return 'REJECTED'
    modulus=p**j
    step=p**(E-s)
    start=x0%step
    hist={}
    for x in range(start,modulus,step):
        y=value(x)%modulus
        hist[y]=hist.get(y,0)+1
    return sorted([y,n] for y,n in hist.items())

cases=json.load(sys.stdin)
assert type(cases) is list and len(cases)<=100
print(json.dumps([solve(c) for c in cases],separators=(',',':')))
```

    "execution_status": "PASS",
    "host_receipt_sha256": "631b976e7954af6f2a01828eb8bb2c4f378198337d09e99ebdfc4898212a43a1",
    "independent_reference": "The uniform distribution among higher value lifts stated for the polynomial argument class.",
    "input_contract": "JSON list of at most 100 cases [ascending integer coefficients, p, x0, E, j]; degree 1 through 3, coefficients and x0 of absolute value at most 5, prime p in {2,3,5}, 0<=E<=5, 0<=j<=6, p**j<=729. Invalid derivative or lifting conditions yield REJECTED.",

    "oracle_code": "import json,sys\nfrom collections import Counter\nfrom sympy import Poly,Symbol\nz=Symbol('z')\ndata=json.loads(open(sys.argv[1]).read())\nwanted=[]\nfor coefficients,prime,origin,level,conductor in data:\n    polynomial=Poly.from_list(list(reversed(coefficients)),gens=z)\n    derivative=int(polynomial.diff().eval(origin))\n    if derivative==0:\n        wanted.append('REJECTED')\n        continue\n    powers=[prime**a for a in range(abs(derivative).bit_length()+1) if derivative%(prime**a)==0]\n    valuation=len(powers)-1\n    if level<=2*valuation or conductor<=level:\n        wanted.append('REJECTED')\n        continue\n    residue=int(polynomial.eval(origin))%(prime**level)\n    prediction=[[residue+t*prime**level,prime**valuation] for t in range(prime**(conductor-level))]\n    actual=Counter(int(polynomial.eval(origin+prime**(level-valuation)*t))%(prime**conductor)\n                   for t in range(prime**(conductor-level+valuation)))\n    observed=sorted([int(a),int(b)] for a,b in actual.items())\n    if observed!=prediction:\n        sys.exit(9)\n    wanted.append(prediction)\ntry:\n    candidate=json.loads(open(sys.argv[2]).read())\n    declared=json.loads(sys.argv[3])\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(7)\nsys.exit(0 if candidate==wanted and declared==wanted else 8)\n",
    "output_contract": "Sparse exact histograms of polynomial values on the argument class, with a rejection marker when the stated lifting conditions fail.",
    "reason": "Uniform lifting on a polynomial argument class is finite and constructive at each fixed prime-power modulus. The executable example checks this local result; the asymptotic density conclusion has separate scope.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of cases [ascending integer coefficients, prime p, x0, E, j].\n# PRECONDITIONS: bounded finite contract; finite derivative valuation s; E >= 2*s+1; j > E.\n# OUTPUTS: sparse exact value histograms, or REJECTED for a failed precondition.\n# TIME: O(sum(p**j * degree)) exact integer operations over admitted cases.\n# SPACE: O(sum(p**j)) integer histogram entries.\n# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpz exact integers; integer modular arithmetic.\n# FAILURE: invalid mathematical preconditions return REJECTED; malformed input raises an error.\nimport json,sys\nfrom flint import fmpz\n\ndef solve(c):\n    coeff,p,x0,E,j=c\n    assert p in (2,3,5) and 2 <= len(coeff) <= 4\n    assert all(type(a) is int and abs(a)<=5 for a in coeff)\n    assert type(x0) is int and abs(x0)<=5\n    assert 0<=E<=5 and 0<=j<=6 and p**j<=729\n    def value(x):\n        acc=fmpz(0)\n        for a in reversed(coeff):\n            acc=acc*x+a\n        return int(acc)\n    der=sum(i*coeff[i]*x0**(i-1) for i in range(1,len(coeff)))\n    if der==0:\n        return 'REJECTED'\n    s=0\n    while der%p==0:\n        der//=p\n        s+=1\n    if E < 2*s+1 or j<=E:\n        return 'REJECTED'\n    modulus=p**j\n    step=p**(E-s)\n    start=x0%step\n    hist={}\n    for x in range(start,modulus,step):\n        y=value(x)%modulus\n        hist[y]=hist.get(y,0)+1\n    return sorted([y,n] for y,n in hist.items())\n\ncases=json.load(sys.stdin)\nassert type(cases) is list and len(cases)<=100\nprint(json.dumps([solve(c) for c in cases],separators=(',',':')))\n",

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A system representing a qualifying integer set may use the theorem as a conditional cardinality constraint. The polynomial must remain fixed, and the polynomial-dependent constant and threshold must be established before a numerical bound is used. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source bounds integer sets with prescribed forbidden differences; it supplies no game dynamics, simulation model, or simulation-error guarantee. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source proves arithmetic bounds and a lifting identity; it supplies no executable tool, interface contract, or tool-performance guarantee. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The local lifting lemma can guide exact residue enumeration: under its derivative valuation hypothesis, polynomial evaluation on the specified argument class has uniform fibers over the allowed value lifts. This is a finite arithmetic component, not a complexity guarantee for finding an avoiding set. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The cardinality bound does not specify a representation, update rule, lookup operation, or storage complexity for an integer set. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The prime-modulus and Fourier estimates do not supply an accelerator design, precision contract, or hardware performance guarantee. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Intersectivity and difference avoidance are arithmetic hypotheses; the source establishes no security game, adversary model, or hardness guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The tuple laws and moment bounds concern arithmetic functions and forbidden differences; they establish no learning objective, training method, or generalization guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The maximal functional bounds a mathematical extremal quantity; the source supplies no optimisation solver, approximation ratio, or operations-research model. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Progression covariance and reflection positivity concern arithmetic laws; they establish no distributed protocol, fault model, or coordination guarantee. |
| other (additive_combinatorics) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | In additive combinatorics, the theorem bounds the size of integer sets avoiding nonzero values of a fixed intersective polynomial. The saving exponent depends only on the degree, while the constant and threshold may depend on the polynomial. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-1** (hypothesis): The density bound requires an intersective integer polynomial of degree $k\ge2$ with positive leading coefficient. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to a polynomial outside its stated hypotheses.']
- **limit-2** (hypothesis): The set must lie in $[N]$ and avoid every nonzero value of the polynomial at positive integer arguments as a difference. Blocked use: ['Replacing polynomial difference avoidance with an unrelated spacing rule.', 'Checking only selected forbidden values without establishing the full avoidance condition.']
- **limit-3** (approximation): The theorem gives a sufficiently-large-scale bound with polynomial-dependent constants and threshold. Blocked use: ['Treating the bound as a calibrated finite-scale capacity estimate without the constants and threshold.']
- **limit-4** (hypothesis): The polynomial remains fixed as the interval grows; only the saving exponent is common to a fixed degree. Blocked use: ['Claiming coefficient-uniform constants.', 'Varying the polynomial with the interval scale under the same constants.']
- **limit-5** (edge_case): Zero polynomial values impose no restriction, and the theorem concerns ordinary integer differences. Blocked use: ['Excluding a set merely because a zero polynomial value occurs.', 'Substituting modular differences for ordinary integer differences.']
- **limit-6** (counterexample_scope): Failure of intersectivity supplies a congruence obstruction: multiples of a modulus without a polynomial root avoid polynomial differences. Blocked use: ['Extending the density conclusion to all nonconstant integer polynomials.']
- **limit-7** (hypothesis): The degree-only local structural choices require the unit condition at the chosen roots, obtained through normalization before transferring the final bound. Blocked use: ['Assuming degree-only local thresholds for arbitrary unnormalized root data.']
- **limit-8** (hypothesis): Uniform lifting requires finite derivative valuation and the displayed lower bound on the lifting exponent. Blocked use: ['Using the lifting assertion at a root with zero derivative without proving a finite valuation.', 'Using a lifting exponent below the stated hypothesis.']
- **limit-9** (hypothesis): Progression covariance requires the progression multiplier to be coprime to the local prime. Blocked use: ['Inverting the progression multiplier at a prime dividing it.']
- **limit-10** (hypothesis): Uniform lift moments require an interval long enough relative to the Fourier cutoff. Blocked use: ['Invoking the moment estimate on a short interval without its length condition.']
- **limit-11** (hypothesis): The cancellation estimate has a restricted auxiliary-index range and requires a good residue assignment. Blocked use: ['Applying cancellation to arbitrary assignments or unrestricted auxiliary indices.']
- **limit-12** (formal_scope): Finite computations of local lifting can check instances of the supporting lemma; they do not establish the asymptotic density theorem. Blocked use: ['Reporting local finite tests as verification of the power-saving density theorem.']
- **limit-13** (edge_case): The polynomial kernel has signed weights, so its support-based cancellation does not assert that it is a probability law. Blocked use: ['Using the signed kernel as a nonnegative sampling distribution.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


An integer polynomial is intersective if it has a root modulo every positive integer. For each degree <i>k</i> ≥ 2, we prove that there is an exponent $`c_k\gt 0`$ such that every set $`A\subseteq\{1,\ldots,N\}`$ whose differences avoid all nonzero values $`h(1),h(2),\ldots`$, where <i>h</i> is an intersective polynomial of degree <i>k</i> with positive leading coefficient, satisfies $`|A|=O_h(N^{1-c_k})`$. The implied constant may depend on <i>h</i>, but the power-saving exponent depends only on its degree.
