   \rho_l\sum_{\substack{\varnothing\ne B\subseteq\mathcal P_N\\q_B\le N^\tau}}
      \eta_{l,B}q_B^s\right)
 \le M_0^s(1-3\rho_*/4).$$ This upper bound tends to $1-3\rho_*/4<1$ as $s$ tends to zero. Two further restrictions handle the other parts of the argument. For any proposed $a>0$, Corollary <a href="#lift:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lift:density">21</a> gives a bound $\mathcal Y_l(N)\le C_aN^a$ for all positive $l,N$. Taking $b=2a/\zeta$ makes $l^bN^{-a}>N^a$ whenever $l>N^\zeta$, so this subpower bound covers the range outside the recurrence after increasing $C$. Finally, $a<\sigma$ makes $C_1N^{-\sigma}=o(N^{-a})$, allowing the additive error to be absorbed at sufficiently large scales.

<a id="induction:exponent-choice"></a>
Choose explicitly $$a_0=\frac12\min\left\{
   \sigma,\quad \frac1{k+2/\zeta},\quad
   \frac{-\log(1-3\rho_*/4)}{(k+2/\zeta)\log M_0}
 \right\},
 \qquad b_0=\frac{2a_0}{\zeta}.$$ These numbers are positive because $M_0\ge2$ and $0<\rho_*\le1$. They satisfy $$a_0<\sigma,\qquad
 s_0:=ka_0+b_0\le1,\qquad
 \theta:=M_0^{s_0}(1-3\rho_*/4)<1.
 \label{induction:exponent-choice}$$ Indeed, $s_0=(k+2/\zeta)a_0\le1/2$, and the third term in the minimum gives $$s_0\log M_0\le-\tfrac12\log(1-3\rho_*/4),
 \qquad
 \theta\le(1-3\rho_*/4)^{1/2}<1.$$ This displays the asserted structural dependence. Under Condition <a href="#local:unit-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="local:unit-condition">[local:unit-condition]</a>, Sections <a href="#sec:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local">2</a> and <a href="#sec:tuples" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tuples">3</a> permit $L,P,M_0,d$ to be fixed in terms of $k$ only. Then $\rho_*=M_0^{1-L}$ and all parameters in <a href="#eq:14" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:14">[eq:14]</a> depend only on $k$, so the displayed choices of $a_0,b_0$ do as well. The constants in the estimates below, and consequently $C_0$, may depend on the fixed data. All quantities in <a href="#induction:exponent-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="induction:exponent-choice">[induction:exponent-choice]</a> are fixed from now on.

<a id="induction:subpower-bound"></a>
Corollary <a href="#lift:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lift:density">21</a> supplies a constant $C_*\ge1$ such that $$\mathcal Y_l(N)\le C_*N^{a_0}
 \qquad(l,N\ge1).
 \label{induction:subpower-bound}$$ If $l>N^\zeta$, then $$l^{b_0}N^{-a_0}>N^{\zeta b_0-a_0}=N^{a_0},$$ so <a href="#eq:33" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:33">[eq:33]</a> follows from <a href="#induction:subpower-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="induction:subpower-bound">[induction:subpower-bound]</a> for any $C_0\ge C_*$. This establishes the estimate outside the range of the recurrence at every scale.

<a id="induction:threshold"></a>
We choose the initial range before fixing $C_0$. Set $\kappa=(1-\theta)/2>0$, and take an integer $N_*\ge N_1$ so large that for every $N\ge N_*$, $$\theta u_N\le\kappa,\qquad
 C_1N^{a_0-\sigma}\le\kappa.
 \label{induction:threshold}$$ This is possible because $u_N\to0$ and $a_0<\sigma$; the choice is independent of $C_0$. Now choose $C_0\ge\max\{1,C_*N_*^{2a_0}\}$. For all $l\ge1$ and $N<N_*$, <a href="#induction:subpower-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="induction:subpower-bound">[induction:subpower-bound]</a> gives $$\mathcal Y_l(N)\le C_*N^{a_0}
 \le C_0N^{-a_0}\le C_0l^{b_0}N^{-a_0}.$$ Thus the initial range is covered simultaneously for every $l$.

Proceed by strong induction on $N$, simultaneously for all positive integers $l$. Suppose $N\ge N_*$ and the estimate is known at every smaller scale. The case $l>N^\zeta$ was established above, so assume $l\le N^\zeta$ and apply Proposition <a href="#induction:recurrence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="induction:recurrence">30</a>. Its successor scales satisfy $1\le n_B<N$, so the simultaneous induction hypothesis gives $\mathcal Y_{lD_B}(n_B)\le C_0(lD_B)^{b_0}n_B^{-a_0}$ at every positive index $lD_B$, with no restriction on $lD_B$ relative to $n_B$. The trial calculation above, with $C=C_0$, $a=a_0$, $b=b_0$, and $s=s_0$, therefore bounds the successor expression inside the parentheses of <a href="#eq:31" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:31">[eq:31]</a> by $\theta C_0l^{b_0}N^{-a_0}$. Substituting in <a href="#eq:31" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:31">[eq:31]</a>, and then using <a href="#induction:threshold" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="induction:threshold">[induction:threshold]</a>, yields $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal Y_l(N)
 &\le C_1N^{-\sigma}
       +(1+u_N)\theta C_0l^{b_0}N^{-a_0}\\
 &\le \kappa N^{-a_0}
       +(1-\kappa)C_0l^{b_0}N^{-a_0}
 \le C_0l^{b_0}N^{-a_0}.
\end{aligned}$$ The last step uses $C_0l^{b_0}\ge1$. This closes the induction. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-induction-decay-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-induction-decay</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Fix $k\ge2$. For this degree, fix the numerical choices furnished under Condition <a href="#local:unit-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="local:unit-condition">[local:unit-condition]</a> by Sections <a href="#sec:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local">2</a>, <a href="#sec:tuples" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tuples">3</a>, and <a href="#sec:lifts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lifts">4</a>. In particular, $L,P,M_0,d,\zeta,\sigma$ and $\rho_*=M_0^{1-L}$ are now fixed using only $k$. Evaluate the explicit minimum above with these numbers to fix $a_0$, and put $$b_0=\frac{2a_0}{\zeta},\qquad c_k=\frac{a_0}{d}\in(0,1).$$ The stated range for $c_k$ follows from $0<a_0<\sigma<1$ and $d\ge1$.

Now let $h\in\mathbb Z[x]$ be an intersective polynomial of degree $k$ with positive leading coefficient, and fix its chosen roots as in Section <a href="#sec:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local">2</a>. Apply Lemma <a href="#local:normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="local:normalization">2</a> to $h$. It supplies positive integers $D_0,M$, an integer $r$ with $-D_0<r\le0$, and an intersective polynomial $$g(x)=\frac{h(r+D_0x)}{M}\in\mathbb Z[x]$$ of degree $k$ with positive leading coefficient whose chosen roots satisfy Condition <a href="#local:unit-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="local:unit-condition">[local:unit-condition]</a>. Moreover, $$Mg(m)=h(r+D_0m),\qquad r+D_0m\ge1\quad(m\in\mathbb N_+).$$

Apply the preceding auxiliary family construction and estimates with $g$ and its chosen roots as the fixed polynomial data, using the numerical choices already fixed for degree $k$. Proposition <a href="#induction:decay" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="induction:decay">31</a> then gives a constant $C_0\ge1$, which may depend on the fixed normalized polynomial, its chosen roots, and the fixed structural choices, for which <a href="#eq:33" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:33">[eq:33]</a> holds with the previously chosen $a_0,b_0$. At $l=1$ the auxiliary polynomial is $g_1=g$, since $r_1=0$ and $\lambda(1)=1$. Thus for every positive integer $n$ and every $B\subseteq[n]$ with $(B-B)\cap g(\mathbb N_+)\subseteq\{0\}$, Corollary <a href="#lift:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lift:density">21</a> and Proposition <a href="#induction:decay" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="induction:decay">31</a>, both applied to the framework for $g$, give $$\frac{|B|}{n}
 \le Y_1(n,B)^{1/d}
 \le C_0^{1/d}n^{-c_k}.$$

Now let $N\ge M$ and let $A\subseteq[N]$ satisfy the avoidance condition of the theorem. For each $j\in[M]$, set $$A_j=\left\{x\in\left[\left\lceil\frac NM\right\rceil\right]:
            j+M(x-1)\in A\right\}.$$ These sets give the progression coordinates of a partition of $A$. If a nonzero difference in $A_j-A_j$ were $g(m)$ for some $m\in\mathbb N_+$, the corresponding difference in $A-A$ would be $Mg(m)=h(r+D_0m)$, nonzero and with $r+D_0m\ge1$, contradicting avoidance. The bound just obtained therefore applies to each $A_j$. Since $\lceil N/M\rceil\le2N/M$ for $N\ge M$ and $1-c_k>0$, it gives $$|A|=\sum_{j=1}^M|A_j|
 \le M C_0^{1/d}\left\lceil\frac NM\right\rceil^{1-c_k}
 \le 2^{1-c_k}M^{c_k}C_0^{1/d}N^{1-c_k}.$$ Taking $C_h=2^{1-c_k}M^{c_k}C_0^{1/d}\ge1$ and $N_h=M$ proves the theorem. The constants $C_h,N_h$ may depend on every coefficient of $h$, while $c_k$ depends only on $k$. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "Regrouping at a fixed new address $u = u_{S∪{b}}(v)$ and restricting the index of one slot $v ∈ V_{+}$. The horizontal arrow only relabels the same independent entries; it incurs no probability factor. The highlighted sublist is prescribed by $v_{b}$. Restricting all $n_{*}$ independent slot indices to their respective prescribed sublists has probability $r_{*}^{−n_{*}}$. The ellipses denote the other words in $Π_{+}$; boxes describe index positions, and their field values may coincide.",
      "figure_id": "tuple:fig-regrouping",
      "pdf_page": 17,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "tuple:fig-regrouping",
          "exact_quote": "Regrouping at a fixed new address\n$u=u_{S\\cup\\{b\\}}(v)$ and restricting the index of one slot $v\\in V_+$.\nThe horizontal arrow only relabels the same independent entries; it\nincurs no probability factor.  The highlighted sublist is prescrib",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n  x=1cm,y=1cm,\n  font=\\small,\n  list/.style={draw=black!65,rounded corners=1pt,\n               minimum width=3.1cm,minimum height=.56cm},\n  selected/.style={fill=blue!9,draw=blue!60!black},\n  every path/.style={line width=.55pt}\n]\n  \\node[align=center] at (1.55,.85)\n    {$r_*$ old plus lists\\\\addresses $(u,q)$, $q\\in\\Pi_+$};\n  \\node[align=center] at (8.65,.85)\n    {one new list at $u$\\\\length $\\ell_{s+1}=r_*\\ell_s$};\n\n  \\node[list] at (1.55,0) {$1\\quad\\cdots\\quad\\ell_s$};\n  \\node[anchor=east] at (-.15,0) {$q_1$};\n  \\node at (1.55,-.62) {$\\vdots$};\n  \\node[list,selected] at (1.55,-1.25)\n    {$1\\quad\\cdots\\quad\\ell_s$};\n  \\node[anchor=east] at (-.15,-1.25) {$q=v_b$};\n  \\node at (1.55,-1.88) {$\\vdots$};\n  \\node[list] at (1.55,-2.5) {$1\\quad\\cdots\\quad\\ell_s$};\n  \\node[anchor=east] at (-.15,-2.5) {$q_{r_*}$};\n\n  \\draw[<->,>=Stealth] (3.38,-1.25)--(6.82,-1.25);\n  \\node[above,align=center] at (5.1,-1.13) {bijective relabeling};\n  \\node[below,align=center] at (5.1,-1.37) {no restriction};\n\n  \\draw[black!65,rounded corners=1pt] (7.05,.32) rectangle (10.25,-2.82);\n  \\node at (8.65,0) {$\\{q_1\\}\\times[\\ell_s]$};\n  \\node at (8.65,-.62) {$\\vdots$};\n  \\filldraw[selected] (7.05,-.97) rectangle (10.25,-1.53);\n  \\node at (8.65,-1.25) {$\\{v_b\\}\\times[\\ell_s]$};\n  \\node at (8.65,-1.88) {$\\vdots$};\n  \\node at (8.65,-2.5) {$\\{q_{r_*}\\}\\times[\\ell_s]$};\n\n  \\draw[->,>=Stealth] (8.65,-2.92)--(8.65,-3.72);\n  \\node[anchor=east,align=right] at (8.45,-3.32)\n    {restrict the index\\\\to $q=v_b$};\n  \\node[anchor=west] at (8.85,-3.32) {probability $1/r_*$};\n  \\node[list,selected] at (8.65,-4.1) {$j'_v=(v_b,i)$};\n  \\node[below] at (8.65,-4.42) {$i$ uniform on $[\\ell_s]$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A diagram showing list relabeling and index restriction.\nLeft side: Headed by \"$r_*$ old plus lists\" and \"addresses $(u, q)$, $q \\in \\Pi_+$\". Shows three horizontal list boxes containing \"$1 \\ \\cdots \\ \\ell_s$\", labeled on the left with $q_1$, $q = v_b$, and $q_{r_*}$, separated by vertical dots. The middle box labeled $q = v_b$ is shaded light blue/purple.\nCenter: A horizontal bidirectional arrow connecting left and right sides, labeled \"bijective relabeling\" on top and \"no restriction\" below.\nRight side: Headed by \"one new list at $u$\" and \"length $\\ell_{s+1} = r_* \\ell_s$\". Shows a single vertical column partitioned into segments labeled $\\{q_1\\} \\times [\\ell_s]$, vertical dots, $\\{v_b\\} \\times [\\ell_s]$ (shaded light blue/purple), vertical dots, and $\\{q_{r_*}\\} \\times [\\ell_s]$.\nBottom right: A vertical downward arrow leads from the shaded $\\{v_b\\} \\times [\\ell_s]$ segment to a box containing \"$j'_v = (v_b, i)$\" (shaded light blue/purple). Beside the arrow is the text \"restrict the index to $q = v_b$\" on the left and \"probability $1/r_*$\" on the right. Below the box is the text \"$i$ uniform on $[\\ell_s]$\"."
    }
  ],

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.edges.v2 -->
```json
{
  "edges": [
    {
      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The polynomial result includes square differences as a qualifying intersective polynomial, with a degree-dependent saving.",
      "relation": "generalises",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-182.1",
      "source_quote": "For every integer $k\\ge2$, there is a constant $c_k\\in(0,1)$ with the following property. For every intersective polynomial $h\\in\\mathbb Z[x]$ of degree $k$ with positive leading coefficient, there are a constant $C_h\\ge1$ and an integer $N_h\\ge1$ such that, whenever $N\\ge N_h$ and $A\\subseteq[N]$ satisfy $$(A-A)\\cap h(\\mathbb N_+)\\subseteq\\{0\\},$$ one has $$|A|\\le C_h N^{1-c_k}.$$",
      "target_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-182.3",
      "target_quote": "There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $C<\\infty$ such that, for every integer $N\\ge1$ and every square-difference-free set $A\\subseteq[N]$, $$|A|\\le C N^{1-c}.$$",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "graph_revision": "store-599",
  "pm_id": "PM-182.1",
  "schema": "pm.edges.v2"
}
```
